# MOTCO case-study report: ADNI serum metabolomics, sex × stage — 2026-11-02, report 2

Generated in full by `adni-sexdiff report` (changes `adni-report`, `adni-report-variants`, `adni-report-stage-arm`, `adni-report-rank-sweep`, `adni-report-nmr-all-columns` and `adni-report-scale-variants`) from the kept aggregate outputs of the pipeline: every number is copied from a record, a manifest or a CSV and formatted with the digits of the summary it belongs to, every reading sentence is the one `summary.md`, `latent_sensitivity.md`, `export_sensitivity.md`, `stage_sensitivity.md` or `rank_sensitivity.md` prints, and the reading rules are the constants, verbatim. The primary analysis stays primary in every table; the arm is read beside it under `READING_RULES` and `LATENT_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`, the export variants `nist_bridge` and `per_run_standardized` under `READING_RULES` and `EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES`, the export variant `nmr_all_columns` under `READING_RULES` and `NMR_MEMBERSHIP_READING_RULES`, the export variants `raw_scale`, `log2_plus_one` and `glog` under `READING_RULES` and `SCALE_VARIANT_READING_RULES`, the CN / LMCI / AD arm under `READING_RULES` and `STAGE_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`, and the rank sweep under `READING_RULES` and `RANK_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12). `adni-sexdiff report --check` compares this file with the outputs cell by cell. No value here is participant-level.

## Summary

The primary analysis (`results/motco_analysis`: one `motco.analysis.analyze_trajectories` call on the covariate-blind basis with the five covariates `age`, `education`, `apoe4`, `phase`, `statin`; MOTCO 0.10.0): n = 1501 in the sex × stage cells Female CN 237 / MCI 310 / AD 125; Male CN 223 / MCI 448 / AD 158; CV-selected PLS-DA rank 8 (`cv`; rank floor `ANALYSIS_RANK_FLOOR` = 2; pooled relative eigengap 0.74399); 9999 RRPP permutations, seed 20261027; α = 0.05, rejected when p <= alpha (`ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE`). `delta` 1.10279 (p 0.1116, not rejected); `angle` 16.5406 (p 0.6879, not rejected); `shape` 0.107355 (p 0.7105, not rejected).

Reading, under `READING_RULES` (section 12):

- `delta` is not rejected: no evidence of a difference between the sexes' trajectories at this design.
- `angle` is not rejected: uninformative about orientation, not evidence that the sexes' directions agree (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape` is not rejected: uninformative about shape, not evidence that the sexes' trajectory shapes agree (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

The predeclared sensitivity arm `latent_stage` (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_ARM`: `covariate_latent_adjustment` = `stage`, the same five covariates, every other setting the primary's; section 7) selected rank 4. Status per statistic (`same` when the rejection under `ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE` equals the primary's, else `differs (basis dependence)`; the primary's status is the result either way), read under `READING_RULES` and `LATENT_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`:

- `delta`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree (no power statement exists at this design).
- `angle`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

Status per statistic (`same` when the rejection under `ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE` equals the primary's, else `differs (run-handling dependence)`; the primary's status is the result either way), read under `READING_RULES` and `EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES`:

The predeclared CN / LMCI / AD arm `cn_lmci_ad` (`STAGE_SENSITIVITY_ARM`, change `adni-stage-sensitivity`: the primary export's rows of the participants whose `DX.bl` is CN, LMCI or AD, staged by `STAGE_RULE`'s map (CN → CN, LMCI → MCI, AD → AD), SMC and EMCI dropped; every other export and analysis setting the primary's; section 9) selected rank 6. Status per statistic (`same` when the rejection under `ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE` equals the primary's, else `differs (stage-definition dependence)`; the primary's status is the result either way), read under `READING_RULES` and `STAGE_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12):

The predeclared rank sweep `forced_rank_ladder` (`RANK_SENSITIVITY_LADDER`, change `adni-rank-sensitivity`: the primary's analysis at the forced ranks 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, `forced_components` set to the rank, every other setting the primary's; the anchor, a forced run at the primary's rank 8, reproduced the primary; section 10). Status per statistic (`same` when the rejection under `ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE` equals the primary's, else `differs (rank dependence)`; the primary's status is the result either way), read under `READING_RULES` and `RANK_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12):

- `delta`: differs (rank dependence) at rank 6, same at ranks 2, 3, 4, 9 and 12 (primary not rejected; rank 6 rejected). A rejection at a forced rank only is rank dependence, not a result at that rank, read beside that rank's null summary; the primary's non-rejection stays the result, and no rule counts the ranks that reject (sweep rules 2, 5 and 6).
- `angle`: same at every rank (primary not rejected; ranks 2, 3, 4, 6, 9 and 12 not rejected). No forced rank rejects where the primary does not; each non-rejection is no more informative than the primary's (sweep rule 5), and none is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same at every rank (primary not rejected; ranks 2, 3, 4, 6, 9 and 12 not rejected). No forced rank rejects where the primary does not; each non-rejection is no more informative than the primary's (sweep rule 5), and none is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

Axis diagnostics (|rho| ≥ 0.3 flagged, a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule): in the primary, 5 latent-component rows flagged on the raw correlation and 1 on the partial, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_3`, `pls_4` (section 6); in the arm, 3 and 1, the flagged components `pls_1`, `pls_2` (section 7); in `nist_bridge`, 1 and 1, the flagged components `pls_1`; in `per_run_standardized`, 2 and 1, the flagged components `pls_1`, `pls_3`; in `nmr_all_columns`, 5 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_4`, `pls_5`; in `raw_scale`, 5 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_3`, `pls_5`; in `log2_plus_one`, 5 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_3`, `pls_4`; in `glog`, 5 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_3`, `pls_4` (section 8); in `cn_lmci_ad`, 4 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_4` (section 9); in the rank sweep's top rank `rank_12`, whose components up to the primary's rank are the primary's (sweep rule 7), 5 and 1, the flagged components `pls_0`, `pls_1`, `pls_3`, `pls_4` (section 10). A flagged row is a measurement-space finding, never a change of a statistic.

## Cohort

- `COHORT_RULE` as `results/cohort/cohort_manifest.json` records it: clinical table `ADNI_adnimerge_20170629_QT-freeze.csv`, one row per participant from its `bl` row; a cleaned participant enters a platform's set only if every raw record on that platform has visit `bl` (`ADMCDUKEP180FIAADNI2GO.csv` column `VISCODE`, `ADMCDUKEP180UPLCADNI2GO.csv` column `VISCODE`, `ADNINIGHTINGALE2.csv` column `VISCODE2`; runs without a visit column count as baseline: ADNI1) and it has a baseline row; the analysis cohort is the intersection of the sets of p180, nmr; no other exclusion.
- `STAGE_RULE`: `DX.bl` on the baseline row, AD → AD, CN → CN, EMCI → MCI, LMCI → MCI, SMC → CN.
- Stage source agreement (`DX` on the baseline row read as Dementia → AD, MCI → MCI, NL → CN; a recorded change 'X to Y' counts as Y; read for the agreement count only): agree 1498, differ 2, missing 1.
- Statin users in the intersection: 704; missing covariate values: 0; phase-against-run disagreements: 0.

## Export

- `EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE` as `results/motco_input/manifest.json` records it: units `log2(x + floor)` (the floor of a metabolite its 0.05 quantile, `linear` interpolation, over the exported participants, computed without sex, stage or a covariate); MOTCO transform `identity`; derived marks excluded from the blocks: nmr: degree (equals `UNSATURATION`), percentage (endswith `_PCT`), ratio (contains `_BY_`), size (endswith `_SIZE`); p180: none. Nothing imputed, replaced, winsorized, removed or centred per run.
- Blocks in MOTCO order: p180, nmr; sample column `rid`, group `sex` (Female, Male), stage `stage` (CN → MCI → AD); covariate columns exported, not applied (`covariates_applied` false): `age`, `education`, `apoe4`, `phase`, `statin`.
- Participants 1501 (expected 1501); joint features 298; missing covariate values 0.

### Features per block

| block | cleaned | kept | derived percentage | derived ratio | derived degree | derived size | share of the joint features | expected kept |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| p180 | 133 | 133 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.446 | 133 |
| nmr | 245 | 165 | 72 | 4 | 1 | 3 | 0.554 | 165 |

The p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO, on the rule's scale over the exported participants), recorded by `export` and not corrected: the runs differ in stage composition, so per-run centring would move stage signal between runs; `phase` enters the analysis as a covariate of the stage means.

## Primary analysis

### Settings

| setting | value | note |
|---|---|---|
| `ANALYSIS_PERMUTATIONS` | 9999 | RRPP permutations; the p-value (1 + b) / (1 + B) is exact in size at any count |
| `ANALYSIS_SEED` | 20261027 | the RRPP seed (one stream per worker) |
| `ANALYSIS_ALPHA` | 0.05 | rejected when p <= alpha (`ANALYSIS_REJECTION_RULE`) |
| `ANALYSIS_RANK_FLOOR` | 2 | `min_components` 2, checked equal to MOTCO's default before any input is read |
| `ANALYSIS_LATENT_ADJUSTMENT` | none | the covariate-blind basis of MOTCO's validated path; the `stage`-conditioned arm is section 7 |
| `ANALYSIS_MISSING_COVARIATES` | error | nothing imputed; a missing value stops the step |
| `n_jobs` | 1 | reported runs use `REPORTED_N_JOBS` = 1 |
| entry point | `motco.analysis.analyze_trajectories` | one call; never assembled from `motco plsr` + `motco de` |
| group order | Female, Male | `sex` |
| stage order | CN → MCI → AD | `stage` |
| categorical covariates | `apoe4`, `phase`, `statin` | `ANALYSIS_CATEGORICAL_COVARIATES`; the others numeric |

MOTCO's other settings stay its defaults and are read back from the record; a moved default is a finding:

### MOTCO defaults used

| default | as run | stated by proposal |
|---|---|---|
| orientation_estimator | net_displacement | net_displacement |
| cv1_splits | 3 | 3 |
| cv2_splits | 4 | 4 |
| n_repeats | 5 | 5 |
| max_components | 20 | 20 |
| random_state | 1203 | 1203 |
| forced_components | n/a | n/a |
| attribution | false | n/a |

MOTCO 0.10.0; orientation `net_displacement`; 9999 RRPP permutations, seed 20261027; n_jobs 1; α = 0.05, rejected when p <= alpha.

### Statistics

| statistic | observed | p (RRPP) | rejected | null mean | null sd | null q95 | note |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| delta | 1.10279 | 0.1116 | no | 0.553618 | 0.423602 | 1.37094 |  |
| angle | 16.5406 | 0.6879 | no | 20.9569 | 7.64839 | 35.4399 | degrees, endpoint stages CN and AD; CV-selected rank 8 (rank floor 2); pooled relative eigengap 0.74399 |
| shape | 0.107355 | 0.7105 | no | 0.164185 | 0.0869842 | 0.322799 |  |

Reading, under the rules of section 12:

- `delta` is not rejected: no evidence of a difference between the sexes' trajectories at this design.
- `angle` is not rejected: uninformative about orientation, not evidence that the sexes' directions agree (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape` is not rejected: uninformative about shape, not evidence that the sexes' trajectory shapes agree (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

| Female | 237 | 310 | 125 |
| Male | 223 | 448 | 158 |

| rank floor (`ANALYSIS_RANK_FLOOR`) | 2 |

The `motco analyze` command that reproduces the directory from its inputs (`cli_equivalent`; replace OUT_DIR with a new directory; paths are relative to the directory analyze ran in):

```
motco analyze --block p180=results/motco_input/p180.csv --block nmr=results/motco_input/nmr.csv --metadata results/motco_analysis/analysis_metadata.csv --sample-col rid --group-col sex --stage-col stage --stage-order CN,MCI,AD --group-order Female,Male --covariate age --covariate education --categorical-covariate apoe4 --categorical-covariate phase --categorical-covariate statin --missing-covariates error --permutations 9999 --seed 20261027 --n-jobs 1 --min-components 2 --out-dir OUT_DIR
```

## Axis diagnostics of the primary

`results/motco_diagnostics` (`axis-diagnostics` on `results/motco_analysis`, rank 8, n = 1501): Spearman correlations of every latent component with the 8 predeclared variables, the partial Spearman correlations given the 6 design columns of the record (age, education, apoe4[1], apoe4[2], phase[ADNI2GO], statin[1]), the same for the first 3 principal components of each z-scored block, and the block composition of each component. Nothing here chooses a variant or an arm; the statistics keep their recorded status.

### Variables (`DIAGNOSTIC_VARIABLES`)

| variable | source | derivation |
|---|---|---|
| `age` | covariates.csv | as exported (years) |
| `education` | covariates.csv | as exported (years) |
| `apoe4` | covariates.csv | APOE e4 allele count 0 / 1 / 2, read as ordinal |
| `phase` | covariates.csv | 1 for ADNI2GO, 0 for ADNI1 (the p180 run; the design column phase[ADNI2GO]) |
| `statin` | covariates.csv | 1 for a statin user at sc, 0 otherwise |
| `stage` | metadata.csv | ordinal CN 0, MCI 1, AD 2 |
| `male` | metadata.csv | 1 for Male, 0 for Female |
| `dx_bl` | cohort.csv | ordinal CN 0, SMC 1, EMCI 2, LMCI 3, AD 4 (ADNI's enrollment gradient) |

Rows with |rho| ≥ 0.3 (`DIAGNOSTIC_CORRELATION_THRESHOLD`, raw and partial separately) are flagged: a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule; no p-value is attached to any correlation. Flagged rows recorded: latent components 5 raw and 1 partial; block principal components 4 raw and 3 partial.

### Flagged latent components

| row | component | sd | variable | source | n | rho | rho \| design | flag |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| pls_0 · phase | pls_0 | 7.334 | `phase` | covariates.csv | 1501 | 0.542 | n/a | raw |
| pls_3 · phase | pls_3 | 3.472 | `phase` | covariates.csv | 1501 | -0.535 | n/a | raw |
| pls_1 · male | pls_1 | 3.901 | `male` | metadata.csv | 1501 | -0.405 | -0.396 | raw+partial |
| pls_4 · statin | pls_4 | 2.54 | `statin` | covariates.csv | 1501 | -0.357 | n/a | raw |
| pls_4 · phase | pls_4 | 2.54 | `phase` | covariates.csv | 1501 | 0.324 | n/a | raw |

### Flagged block principal components

| row | block | component | variance share | variable | source | n | rho | rho \| design | flag |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| p180 pc1 · phase | p180 | pc1 | 0.418 | `phase` | covariates.csv | 1501 | -0.757 | n/a | raw |
| p180 pc2 · phase | p180 | pc2 | 0.071 | `phase` | covariates.csv | 1501 | -0.400 | n/a | raw |
| p180 pc2 · male | p180 | pc2 | 0.071 | `male` | metadata.csv | 1501 | 0.390 | 0.391 | raw+partial |
| nmr pc2 · male | nmr | pc2 | 0.247 | `male` | metadata.csv | 1501 | -0.383 | -0.371 | raw+partial |
| p180 pc1 · male | p180 | pc1 | 0.418 | `male` | metadata.csv | 1501 | -0.190 | -0.347 | partial |

Per latent component: the score SD, the share of its squared `pls_loadings.csv` entries (MOTCO's rotation weights on the z-scored joint features; the blocks enter unweighted) held by each block, the variables flagged on the raw and on the partial correlation, and the variable with the largest |rho|.

### Components

| component | sd | share p180 | share nmr | flagged raw | flagged partial | top variable | top rho |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| pls_0 | 7.334 | 0.567 | 0.433 | 1 | 0 | `phase` | 0.542 |
| pls_1 | 3.901 | 0.635 | 0.365 | 1 | 1 | `male` | -0.405 |
| pls_2 | 5.566 | 0.599 | 0.401 | 0 | 0 | `male` | -0.219 |

Per z-scored block: the variance share of each of its first principal components and its pc1 against `phase` (Spearman here; the export manifest's |Pearson| of the first left-singular vector against the ADNI1 indicator, `counts.phase_offset.<block>.abs_corr_pc1_phase`).

### Block principal components

| block | features | variance share pc1 | variance share pc2 | variance share pc3 | pc1 against phase: Spearman (here) | \|Pearson\| recorded by export |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

## Latent-adjustment sensitivity

The primary (`results/motco_analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `d4a5cbf90dda74763dea91b9f9be8df47439da0e5fd4c475a8546fe2a6f581f0`) beside its predeclared sensitivity arm `latent_stage` (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_ARM`, change `adni-latent-sensitivity`: `covariate_latent_adjustment` = `stage`, the same five covariates, every other setting the primary's; `results/motco_latent_sensitivity/analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `f24e0e7417af6faa8a54ea46cd275aba221c5c94a5c7978f4f583de0a9fb3f70`), as `results/motco_latent_sensitivity/comparison/latent_sensitivity_manifest.json` records them. Every table in this section quotes the arm and is read under `READING_RULES` and `LATENT_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; the arm never replaces it (arm rule 1), and a status that differs is basis dependence (arm rule 2).

### Statistics: primary and arm

`angle` (degrees, endpoint stages CN and AD) is read beside each analysis' CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and null summary (reading rule 2, arm rule 3): primary rank 8 (floor 2, pooled relative eigengap 0.74399); latent_stage rank 4 (floor 2, eigengap 0.878123); the ranks differ, which is reported, not ranked as better or worse (arm rule 3).

Status per statistic:

- `delta`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree (no power statement exists at this design).
- `angle`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same (primary not rejected, latent_stage not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is weaker than the primary's (arm rule 5), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

### Latent space: primary and arm

| quantity | primary | latent_stage |
|---|---:|---:|
| PLS-DA rank (selection) | 8 (`cv`) | 4 (`cv`) |
| rank floor (`ANALYSIS_RANK_FLOOR`) | 2 | 2 |
| cv_mean_auroc | 0.599514 | 0.580311 |
| CV repeats completed | 5 | 5 |
| pooled relative eigengap | 0.74399 | 0.878123 |
| eigengap permutation null q05 / q50 / q95 | 0.537295 / 0.733669 / 0.855842 | 0.632903 / 0.875739 / 0.975946 |
| latent adjustment | none | `stage` |
| residual df | n/a | 1492 |
| smallest CV training df | n/a | 741 |

The two analyses share the export, the covariates and their design, the cells, the CV folds (stratified on the stage, as the primary's), the seed and the permutations; they differ only in the latent basis and the CV-selected rank (arm rule 2).

### Latent adjustment of the arm

- conditioning: `stage` (`covariate_latent_adjustment`, `LATENT_SENSITIVITY_ARM`): the basis is fitted on the pooled-standardized features with the covariates regressed out given the intercept and the stage indicators, per CV training set and on the full sample; the raw features are projected onto it; the trajectory model, LS-means and RRPP are the primary's
- covariates residualized (in order): `age`, `education`, `apoe4`, `phase`, `statin`
- residualization design columns: 8 (stage[1] = MCI, stage[2] = AD, age, education, apoe4[1], apoe4[2], phase[ADNI2GO], statin[1])
- samples 1501; residual df 1492 (n − 1 − 2 stage indicator(s) − 6 design column(s)); smallest CV training df 741
- feature scale: `pooled_standardized`
- feature R² of the covariates given the intercept and the stage indicators (the share of each feature's variance the residualization removed; arm rule 6): q05 / q25 / q50 / q75 / q95 0.015 / 0.034 / 0.116 / 0.258 / 0.486; mean 0.166; fraction above 0.5 0.047

Each covariate's relation to the stage effect (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_COVARIATE_ROLES`, decided before any run; no statistic or threshold is computed for it and nothing changes because of it), read beside the per-cell descriptives of section 3. Residualizing a covariate that mediates the stage effect removes real stage signal, and no data can tell a confounder from a mediator (arm rule 7).

### Covariate roles

| covariate | role | variable | justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| `age` | pre-disease | age at baseline | precedes disease: it can confound the stage effect but cannot be downstream of it |
| `education` | pre-disease | years of schooling, completed before enrolment | precedes disease: it can confound the stage effect but cannot be downstream of it |
| `apoe4` | pre-disease | germline APOE e4 genotype (allele count) | precedes disease: it can confound the stage effect but cannot be downstream of it |
| `phase` | design | the assay run and enrolment protocol (p180 kits ADNI-1 against ADNI-GO/2) | tied to stage composition by design (SMC and EMCI exist only in ADNI-GO/2), not caused by stage |
| `statin` | mediator candidate | HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor use on the screening medication row | a treatment that may follow diagnosis: prescribing differs with cognitive status; it stays in the arm because the arm was predeclared with the five covariates, and the reading carries the limitation |

MOTCO's two latent-adjustment decisions, as the comparison manifest records them (measured at one design, n = 80, none at this one; the arm carries no Type I or power claim of its own, arm rule 8):

- `adopt_stage` (`../MOTCO/docs/reports/latent-adjustment-type-i-2026-10-20.md`): the `stage`-conditioned adjustment holds Type I at the validated design under conditions this change meets: the case study's own proposal justifies the covariate set (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_COVARIATE_ROLES`), `group_stage` is not used, the record's `latent_adjustment` and `covariates` blocks are reported beside the results, no categorical level is rare enough to drop out of a CV training set, and the earlier binding statements stand; `angle` held the 0.0706 bound by 0.004 (0.067).
- `candidate_fails` (`../MOTCO/docs/reports/latent-adjustment-power-2026-10-20.md`): the adjustment loses `shape` power with a covariate-aligned nuisance axis (0.243 against 0.428) and small-magnitude `delta` power without one (0.400 against 0.520 at e = 0.1), so it is a sensitivity read beside the covariate-blind primary, never a replacement of it.

Axis diagnostics of the arm (`results/motco_latent_sensitivity/diagnostics`, rank 4, `is_primary` false): a component of the arm that tracks a variable here is a measurement-space finding about the arm; it never reinterprets either analysis' statistics and is never grounds to adopt the arm (arm rule 9).

Rows with |rho| ≥ 0.3 (`DIAGNOSTIC_CORRELATION_THRESHOLD`, raw and partial separately) are flagged: a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule; no p-value is attached to any correlation. Flagged rows recorded: latent components 3 raw and 1 partial; block principal components 4 raw and 3 partial.

The `motco analyze` command that reproduces the arm's directory (`cli_equivalent`; replace OUT_DIR with a new directory; paths are relative to the directory analyze ran in):

```
motco analyze --block p180=results/motco_input/p180.csv --block nmr=results/motco_input/nmr.csv --metadata results/motco_latent_sensitivity/analysis/analysis_metadata.csv --sample-col rid --group-col sex --stage-col stage --stage-order CN,MCI,AD --group-order Female,Male --covariate age --covariate education --categorical-covariate apoe4 --categorical-covariate phase --categorical-covariate statin --missing-covariates error --latent-adjustment stage --permutations 9999 --seed 20261027 --n-jobs 1 --min-components 2 --out-dir OUT_DIR
```

## Export sensitivity

The primary (`results/motco_analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `d4a5cbf90dda74763dea91b9f9be8df47439da0e5fd4c475a8546fe2a6f581f0`) beside its predeclared export variants (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`), in one group per rule set (`EXPORT_VARIANT_RULE_SETS`), in registry order: `nist_bridge` and `per_run_standardized` under `EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES`; `nmr_all_columns` under `NMR_MEMBERSHIP_READING_RULES`; `raw_scale`, `log2_plus_one` and `glog` under `SCALE_VARIANT_READING_RULES` (section 12). Each group's tables hold the primary and that group's variants only, the primary row first and its status the result.

The primary beside its predeclared export variants (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`): `nist_bridge` (change `adni-nist-bridge`: every cleaned p180 concentration of a participant whose run in p180_runs.csv is ADNI2GO multiplied by 2^b of its metabolite under NIST_BRIDGE_RULE before the floors; EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE then unchanged, its floors recomputed over the exported participants; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge`); `per_run_standardized` (change `adni-nist-bridge`: the p180 values on the scale of EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE (pooled floors, log2(x + floor)), each metabolite centred and divided by its population SD within its run of p180_runs.csv; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized`). Every other export and analysis setting is the primary's. Every table of this group quotes a variant and is read under `READING_RULES` and `EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; a variant never replaces it (variant rule 1), and a status that differs is run-handling dependence (variant rule 2).

The full table `bridge/nist_bridge.csv` (sha256 `bcfcf7460a6fb049f89cbe2f39b4c009d1214074fa7e4ab24a3b037b76b87fd3`, 133 rows) is referenced by hash (section 2); the factor of every metabolite is in the variant export manifest (`bridge.factors_log2`).

### Export variant `per_run_standardized`

- variant: `per_run_standardized` (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`, change `adni-nist-bridge`): the p180 values on the scale of EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE (pooled floors, log2(x + floor)), each metabolite centred and divided by its population SD within its run of p180_runs.csv
- read beside the primary export `results/motco_input` (`manifest.json` sha256 `9f4adf8cbc1da0b4cacdfe6673479991b40676033c365bc1433cc382f67d7204`); the participants, the NMR block, the metadata and the covariates are the primary's
- assay run of the exported participants (`p180_runs.csv`): ADNI1 684, ADNI2GO 817; 0 differ from `phase`
- p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 1.12, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40; variant median |d| 0.00, |d| > 0.5 for 0 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.00, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.00
- nmr run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07; variant median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07

Axis diagnostics of `nist_bridge` (`results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/diagnostics`, rank 6, `is_primary` false, export variant `nist_bridge`): a component of the variant that tracks a variable here is a measurement-space finding about the variant; it never reinterprets either analysis' statistics and is never grounds to adopt the variant (variant rule 7).

Rows with |rho| ≥ 0.3 (`DIAGNOSTIC_CORRELATION_THRESHOLD`, raw and partial separately) are flagged: a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule; no p-value is attached to any correlation. Flagged rows recorded: latent components 1 raw and 1 partial; block principal components 4 raw and 3 partial.

The `motco analyze` command that reproduces `nist_bridge`'s analysis directory (`cli_equivalent`; replace OUT_DIR with a new directory; paths are relative to the directory analyze ran in):

```
motco analyze --block p180=results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/motco_input/p180.csv --block nmr=results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/motco_input/nmr.csv --metadata results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/analysis/analysis_metadata.csv --sample-col rid --group-col sex --stage-col stage --stage-order CN,MCI,AD --group-order Female,Male --covariate age --covariate education --categorical-covariate apoe4 --categorical-covariate phase --categorical-covariate statin --missing-covariates error --permutations 9999 --seed 20261027 --n-jobs 1 --min-components 2 --out-dir OUT_DIR
```

The `motco analyze` command that reproduces `per_run_standardized`'s analysis directory (`cli_equivalent`; replace OUT_DIR with a new directory; paths are relative to the directory analyze ran in):

```
motco analyze --block p180=results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/motco_input/p180.csv --block nmr=results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/motco_input/nmr.csv --metadata results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/analysis/analysis_metadata.csv --sample-col rid --group-col sex --stage-col stage --stage-order CN,MCI,AD --group-order Female,Male --covariate age --covariate education --categorical-covariate apoe4 --categorical-covariate phase --categorical-covariate statin --missing-covariates error --permutations 9999 --seed 20261027 --n-jobs 1 --min-components 2 --out-dir OUT_DIR
```

The primary beside its predeclared export variant (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`): `nmr_all_columns` (change `adni-nmr-all-columns`: every cleaned NMR column enters the block (245): the 165 concentrations of EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE and its 80 derived columns (72 percentages, 4 ratios, the degree of unsaturation, 3 sizes), each on the rule's scale, log2(x + floor) with its own 5 % floor over the exported participants; the p180 block, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/nmr_all_columns`). Every other export and analysis setting is the primary's. Every table of this group quotes the variant and is read under `READING_RULES` and `NMR_MEMBERSHIP_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; the variant never replaces it (membership rule 1), a status that differs is feature-membership dependence (membership rule 2), and the variant is read against the primary only (membership rule 8).

### nmr_all_columns: Statistics: primary and variant

| row | statistic | analysis | observed | p (RRPP) | rejected | null mean | null sd | null q95 | status |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| delta primary | delta | primary | 1.10279 | 0.1116 | no | 0.553618 | 0.423602 | 1.37094 | the result |
| delta nmr_all_columns | delta | nmr_all_columns | 1.53173 | 0.0447 | yes | 0.608253 | 0.462622 | 1.49652 | differs (feature-membership dependence) |
| angle primary | angle | primary | 16.5406 | 0.6879 | no | 20.9569 | 7.64839 | 35.4399 | the result |
| angle nmr_all_columns | angle | nmr_all_columns | 15.9469 | 0.6815 | no | 19.9159 | 7.13512 | 32.9005 | same |
| shape primary | shape | primary | 0.107355 | 0.7105 | no | 0.164185 | 0.0869842 | 0.322799 | the result |
| shape nmr_all_columns | shape | nmr_all_columns | 0.0369556 | 0.9533 | no | 0.155629 | 0.0816776 | 0.30457 | same |

`angle` (degrees, endpoint stages CN and AD) is read beside each analysis' CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and null summary (reading rule 2, membership rule 3): primary rank 8 (floor 2, pooled relative eigengap 0.74399); nmr_all_columns rank 7 (floor 2, eigengap 0.724248); the ranks differ, which is reported, not ranked (membership rule 3).

### Status per statistic: `nmr_all_columns`

- `delta`: differs (feature-membership dependence) (primary not rejected, nmr_all_columns rejected). The variant rejects where the primary does not: dependence on the NMR feature membership, not a result of the variant, read beside the variant's rank and null summary; the primary's non-rejection stays the result (membership rules 2 and 6).
- `angle`: same (primary not rejected, nmr_all_columns not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the variant's is no more informative than the primary's (membership rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same (primary not rejected, nmr_all_columns not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the variant's is no more informative than the primary's (membership rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

The primary beside its predeclared export variants (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`): `raw_scale` (change `adni-scale-variants`: both blocks on the raw scale: every kept cell the cleaned concentration as metabo_adni leaves it, in its platform's unit, with no log and no offset (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); MOTCO's transform identity and its z-scoring; the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale`); `log2_plus_one` (change `adni-scale-variants`: both blocks on log2(x + 1): every kept cell log2 of the cleaned concentration plus 1 in its platform's unit, the transform of the 2022 study (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/log2_plus_one`); `glog` (change `adni-scale-variants`: both blocks on the generalized log log2((x + sqrt(x² + λ²)) / 2), λ per metabolite its smallest positive value over the exported participants divided by 10 (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied; `results/motco_export_sensitivity/glog`). Every other export and analysis setting is the primary's. Every table of this group quotes a variant and is read under `READING_RULES` and `SCALE_VARIANT_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; a variant never replaces it (scale rule 1), a status that differs is scale dependence (scale rule 2), and each variant is read against the primary only, never against another variant of the group (scale rule 8).

### Status per statistic: `raw_scale`

- `delta`: same (primary not rejected, raw_scale not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the variant's is no more informative than the primary's (scale rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree (no power statement exists at this design).
- `angle`: same (primary not rejected, raw_scale not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the variant's is no more informative than the primary's (scale rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same (primary not rejected, raw_scale not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the variant's is no more informative than the primary's (scale rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

### Export variant `raw_scale`

- variant: `raw_scale` (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`, change `adni-scale-variants`): both blocks on the raw scale: every kept cell the cleaned concentration as metabo_adni leaves it, in its platform's unit, with no log and no offset (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); MOTCO's transform identity and its z-scoring; the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied
- read beside the primary export `results/motco_input` (`manifest.json` sha256 `9f4adf8cbc1da0b4cacdfe6673479991b40676033c365bc1433cc382f67d7204`); the participants, the membership of both blocks, the metadata and the covariates are the primary's
- assay run of the exported participants (`p180_runs.csv`): ADNI1 684, ADNI2GO 817; 0 differ from `phase`
- scale (hook `scale`, `SCALE_VARIANT_RULE`): every kept cell of both blocks on `x` in place of the rule's `log2(x + floor)`, with no offset; the primary's membership; the rule's floor computed, gated and recorded per metabolite (the feature tables' `floor`), not applied
- p180 block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 1 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 0, numerical rank 133 of 133; variant (`x`) |skew| > 1 for 46 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 12, numerical rank 133 of 133
- nmr block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 3 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 1, numerical rank 165 of 165; variant (`x`) |skew| > 1 for 43 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 4, numerical rank 104 of 165
- p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 1.12, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40; variant median |d| 1.08, |d| > 0.5 for 108 and > 1 for 76 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.72, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40
- nmr run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07; variant median |d| 0.19, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.06

### Export variant `log2_plus_one`

- variant: `log2_plus_one` (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`, change `adni-scale-variants`): both blocks on log2(x + 1): every kept cell log2 of the cleaned concentration plus 1 in its platform's unit, the transform of the 2022 study (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied
- read beside the primary export `results/motco_input` (`manifest.json` sha256 `9f4adf8cbc1da0b4cacdfe6673479991b40676033c365bc1433cc382f67d7204`); the participants, the membership of both blocks, the metadata and the covariates are the primary's
- assay run of the exported participants (`p180_runs.csv`): ADNI1 684, ADNI2GO 817; 0 differ from `phase`
- scale (hook `scale`, `SCALE_VARIANT_RULE`): every kept cell of both blocks on `log2(x + 1)` in place of the rule's `log2(x + floor)`, offset 1 in each platform's unit (the feature tables' `offset`); the primary's membership; the rule's floor computed, gated and recorded per metabolite (the feature tables' `floor`), not applied
- metabolites whose median lies below the offset 1 (a nearly linear scale; well above it, nearly `log2(x)`): p180 46 of 133, nmr 129 of 165
- p180 block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 1 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 0, numerical rank 133 of 133; variant (`log2(x + 1)`) |skew| > 1 for 14 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 9, numerical rank 133 of 133
- nmr block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 3 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 1, numerical rank 165 of 165; variant (`log2(x + 1)`) |skew| > 1 for 30 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 4, numerical rank 165 of 165
- p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 1.12, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40; variant median |d| 1.11, |d| > 0.5 for 108 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.39
- nmr run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07; variant median |d| 0.20, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07

### Export variant `glog`

- variant: `glog` (`EXPORT_VARIANTS`, change `adni-scale-variants`): both blocks on the generalized log log2((x + sqrt(x² + λ²)) / 2), λ per metabolite its smallest positive value over the exported participants divided by 10 (SCALE_VARIANT_RULE); the membership, the participants, the metadata and the covariates the primary's; the rule's floors recorded, not applied
- read beside the primary export `results/motco_input` (`manifest.json` sha256 `9f4adf8cbc1da0b4cacdfe6673479991b40676033c365bc1433cc382f67d7204`); the participants, the membership of both blocks, the metadata and the covariates are the primary's
- assay run of the exported participants (`p180_runs.csv`): ADNI1 684, ADNI2GO 817; 0 differ from `phase`
- scale (hook `scale`, `SCALE_VARIANT_RULE`): every kept cell of both blocks on `log2((x + sqrt(x² + λ²)) / 2)` in place of the rule's `log2(x + floor)`, λ per metabolite its smallest positive value over the exported participants divided by 10 (the feature tables' `offset`), so a zero cell lands at `log2(λ / 2)`; the primary's membership; the rule's floor computed, gated and recorded per metabolite (the feature tables' `floor`), not applied
- λ over the metabolite's median, q05 / q25 / q50 / q75 / q95: p180 0.00499 / 0.0254 / 0.0335 / 0.0394 / 0.0493; nmr 0.0001 / 0.00893 / 0.035 / 0.0477 / 0.0596
- p180 block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 1 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 0, numerical rank 133 of 133; variant (`log2((x + sqrt(x² + λ²)) / 2)`) |skew| > 1 for 6 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 6, numerical rank 133 of 133
- nmr block shape and numerical rank: primary (`log2(x + floor)`) |skew| > 1 for 3 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 1, numerical rank 165 of 165; variant (`log2((x + sqrt(x² + λ²)) / 2)`) |skew| > 1 for 25 metabolite(s), largest |z| > 8 for 13, numerical rank 165 of 165
- p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 1.12, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40; variant median |d| 1.13, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40
- nmr run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07; variant median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.08

Axis diagnostics of `raw_scale` (`results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/diagnostics`, rank 7, `is_primary` false, export variant `raw_scale`): a component of the variant that tracks a variable here is a measurement-space finding about the variant; it never reinterprets either analysis' statistics and is never grounds to adopt the variant (scale rule 7).

Rows with |rho| ≥ 0.3 (`DIAGNOSTIC_CORRELATION_THRESHOLD`, raw and partial separately) are flagged: a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule; no p-value is attached to any correlation. Flagged rows recorded: latent components 5 raw and 1 partial; block principal components 4 raw and 3 partial.

The `motco analyze` command that reproduces `raw_scale`'s analysis directory (`cli_equivalent`; replace OUT_DIR with a new directory; paths are relative to the directory analyze ran in):

```
motco analyze --block p180=results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/motco_input/p180.csv --block nmr=results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/motco_input/nmr.csv --metadata results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/analysis/analysis_metadata.csv --sample-col rid --group-col sex --stage-col stage --stage-order CN,MCI,AD --group-order Female,Male --covariate age --covariate education --categorical-covariate apoe4 --categorical-covariate phase --categorical-covariate statin --missing-covariates error --permutations 9999 --seed 20261027 --n-jobs 1 --min-components 2 --out-dir OUT_DIR
```

## Stage-definition sensitivity

The primary (`results/motco_analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `d4a5cbf90dda74763dea91b9f9be8df47439da0e5fd4c475a8546fe2a6f581f0`) beside the CN / LMCI / AD arm `cn_lmci_ad` (`STAGE_SENSITIVITY_ARM`, change `adni-stage-sensitivity`: the primary export's rows of the participants whose `DX.bl` is CN, LMCI or AD, staged by `STAGE_RULE`'s map (CN → CN, LMCI → MCI, AD → AD), SMC and EMCI dropped; every other export and analysis setting the primary's; `results/motco_stage_sensitivity/analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `caabf0935fdee8ee31c4d048a4ff2cfa99c3378f0c05209f004b252c98af1b04` as its `analysis_manifest.json` records it), as `results/motco_stage_sensitivity/comparison/stage_sensitivity_manifest.json` records them. Every table in this section quotes the arm and is read under `READING_RULES` and `STAGE_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; the arm never replaces it (stage-arm rule 1), a status that differs is dependence on the stage definition and the sample (stage-arm rule 2), and the arm is read against the primary only (stage-arm rule 8).

### Statistics: primary and arm

| row | statistic | analysis | observed | p (RRPP) | rejected | null mean | null sd | null q95 | status |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| delta primary | delta | primary | 1.10279 | 0.1116 | no | 0.553618 | 0.423602 | 1.37094 | the result |
| delta cn_lmci_ad | delta | cn_lmci_ad | 0.882188 | 0.188 | no | 0.532328 | 0.4092 | 1.31619 | same |
| angle primary | angle | primary | 16.5406 | 0.6879 | no | 20.9569 | 7.64839 | 35.4399 | the result |
| angle cn_lmci_ad | angle | cn_lmci_ad | 20.7602 | 0.5702 | no | 23.362 | 8.70311 | 39.0413 | same |
| shape primary | shape | primary | 0.107355 | 0.7105 | no | 0.164185 | 0.0869842 | 0.322799 | the result |
| shape cn_lmci_ad | shape | cn_lmci_ad | 0.0251113 | 0.9853 | no | 0.188005 | 0.0982976 | 0.365394 | same |

`angle` (degrees, endpoint stages CN and AD) is read beside each analysis' CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and null summary (reading rule 2, stage-arm rule 3): primary rank 8 (floor 2, pooled relative eigengap 0.74399); cn_lmci_ad rank 6 (floor 2, eigengap 0.863574); the ranks differ, which is reported, not ranked (stage-arm rule 3).

Status per statistic:

- `delta`: same (primary not rejected, cn_lmci_ad not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is no more informative than the primary's (stage-arm rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree (no power statement exists at this design).
- `angle`: same (primary not rejected, cn_lmci_ad not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is no more informative than the primary's (stage-arm rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same (primary not rejected, cn_lmci_ad not rejected). Both are non-rejections; the arm's is no more informative than the primary's (stage-arm rule 6), and neither is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

- arm: `cn_lmci_ad` (`STAGE_SENSITIVITY_ARM`, change `adni-stage-sensitivity`): the primary export's rows of the participants whose `DX.bl` is CN, LMCI or AD, staged by `STAGE_RULE`'s map (CN → CN, LMCI → MCI, AD → AD), SMC and EMCI dropped
- read beside the primary export `results/motco_input` (`manifest.json` sha256 `9f4adf8cbc1da0b4cacdfe6673479991b40676033c365bc1433cc382f67d7204`): the arm's rows are its rows, every cell unchanged, so the floors are the primary's (`features_nmr.csv` sha256 `d4495f6aae38985460a118582e8e3250f9c56b8e27fc38d64bedc5b14514b9ee`, `features_p180.csv` sha256 `7793776534d580f9a49e0ad95e0d8f6ecef3b5a7ff3c02f64dd07c905f4bb5ee`); rows equal to the primary's: p180.csv yes, nmr.csv yes, metadata.csv yes, covariates.csv yes
- participants: 1130 of the primary's 1501; dropped: SMC 96, EMCI 275
- p180 run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 1.12, |d| > 0.5 for 109 and > 1 for 80 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.73, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.40; arm median |d| 1.10, |d| > 0.5 for 107 and > 1 for 77 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.71, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.42
- nmr run offset (`phase`, ADNI1 against ADNI2GO): primary median |d| 0.21, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.13, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.07; arm median |d| 0.26, |d| > 0.5 for 2 and > 1 for 0 metabolite(s), |corr(PC1, phase)| 0.15, |corr(PC2, phase)| 0.11

## Rank sensitivity

The primary (`results/motco_analysis`, `result.json` sha256 `d4a5cbf90dda74763dea91b9f9be8df47439da0e5fd4c475a8546fe2a6f581f0`, rank 8 selected by its cross-validation) beside the rank sweep `forced_rank_ladder` (`RANK_SENSITIVITY_LADDER`, change `adni-rank-sensitivity`: the primary's analysis at each forced rank 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, `forced_components` set to the rank and MOTCO's cross-validation skipped; ANALYSIS_SEED and ANALYSIS_PERMUTATIONS at every rank (the same permutation indices at n_jobs = 1); every other export and analysis setting the primary's; `results/motco_rank_sensitivity`), as `results/motco_rank_sensitivity/comparison/rank_sensitivity_manifest.json` records them. Every table in this section quotes the sweep and is read under `READING_RULES` and `RANK_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES` (section 12): the primary row comes first and its status is the result; no rank is chosen, adopted or ranked (sweep rule 1), a status that differs is rank dependence (sweep rule 2), the sweep is not a test (sweep rule 6), and it is read against the primary only (sweep rule 8).

`angle` (degrees, endpoint stages CN and AD) is read beside each analysis' rank, floor (2), eigengap and its own null summary (reading rule 2, sweep rule 4; the latent spaces below), never against the `angle` of another rank.

Status per statistic:

- `delta`: differs (rank dependence) at rank 6, same at ranks 2, 3, 4, 9 and 12 (primary not rejected; rank 6 rejected). A rejection at a forced rank only is rank dependence, not a result at that rank, read beside that rank's null summary; the primary's non-rejection stays the result, and no rule counts the ranks that reject (sweep rules 2, 5 and 6).
- `angle`: same at every rank (primary not rejected; ranks 2, 3, 4, 6, 9 and 12 not rejected). No forced rank rejects where the primary does not; each non-rejection is no more informative than the primary's (sweep rule 5), and none is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).
- `shape`: same at every rank (primary not rejected; ranks 2, 3, 4, 6, 9 and 12 not rejected). No forced rank rejects where the primary does not; each non-rejection is no more informative than the primary's (sweep rule 5), and none is evidence that the sexes' trajectories agree in this respect (reading rule 3: no power statement exists at this design).

The ranks share the primary export, the covariates and their design, the cells, the seed and the permutations; at n_jobs = 1 every rank permutes the same residual indices (sweep rule 2). They differ only in the latent rank: MOTCO skips the cross-validation at a forced rank, so `cv_mean_auroc` is not available and no CV repeat runs. Each rank's `pls_loadings.csv` (MOTCO's rotations) is its own and is not nested; it and the rank's `stage_means.csv` are recorded by hash in the rank's `analysis_manifest.json` (`outputs`), whose own hash is in section 2.

### Anchor and nesting

- anchor (sweep rule 3): a forced run at the primary's rank 8 reproduced the primary before any rank ran: `statistics`, `p_values`, `null_summary`, `config_spectrum`, `selected_lv` equal to the primary record's, and `latent_scores.csv`, `pls_loadings.csv`, `stage_means.csv` hash to the values the primary manifest records; its directory was removed. Predeclared anchor rank 8 (`RANK_SENSITIVITY_EXPECTED_COUNTS`); the primary record's 8.
- nesting (sweep rule 3): PLS-DA components are nested, so each rank's latent scores are the first columns of rank 12's, and the ranks at or below the primary's 8 are the primary's first components (through the anchor, equal to the primary by the gate). The check compares the scores in memory; no score is printed or written by it.

Axis diagnostics of the top rank (`results/motco_rank_sensitivity/diagnostics`, `axis-diagnostics` on `results/motco_rank_sensitivity/rank_12`, rank 12, forced rank 12 of the sweep beside the primary's rank 8, `is_primary` false), the sweep's predeclared diagnostic: PLS-DA components are nested, so its components up to the primary's rank are the primary's, with the primary's correlations (section 6), and a component above it that tracks a variable is a measurement-space finding about the higher ranks and never grounds to choose a rank (sweep rule 7). Flagged components after the primary's first 8, in the order of `components.csv`: none.

Rows with |rho| ≥ 0.3 (`DIAGNOSTIC_CORRELATION_THRESHOLD`, raw and partial separately) are flagged: a reporting threshold, not an adoption rule; no p-value is attached to any correlation. Flagged rows recorded: latent components 5 raw and 1 partial; block principal components 4 raw and 3 partial.

### report

- clean p180 (results/cleaned/p180/clean_manifest.json): no `numerics` block, written before `adni-portable-numerics`
- clean nmr (results/cleaned/nmr/clean_manifest.json): no `numerics` block, written before `adni-portable-numerics`
- cohort (results/cohort/cohort_manifest.json): no `numerics` block, written before `adni-portable-numerics`
- export (results/motco_input/manifest.json): no `numerics` block, written before `adni-portable-numerics`
- analyze (results/motco_analysis/analysis_manifest.json): no `numerics` block, written before `adni-portable-numerics`

## Reading rules

### READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of the result (`READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-analyze` before any run):

1. The primary result is the covariate-adjusted analysis on the covariate-blind basis with the five covariates; every sensitivity (latent adjustment, export variants, the CN / LMCI / AD arm, a rank sweep) is read beside it, and a rejection status that differs in an arm is dependence on that arm's setting, never a replacement of the primary.
2. The CV-selected rank, the rank floor, the record's `config_spectrum` eigengap and the `angle` null summary are reported beside `angle`. The floor defines `angle` and `shape` in at least two dimensions; it does not make them powerful.
3. `angle` and `shape` non-rejections are uninformative: MOTCO's operating characteristics were measured at n = 80 on a structural analogue (InterSIM blocks, 498 features), and no power statement exists at this design (n = 1501, 298 metabolites, six design columns). `angle` reads only the endpoint stages (CN and AD). A rejection is evidence that the trajectories differ in that respect, with Type I controlled at the validated designs.
4. A `delta` rejection is evidence of a difference between the sexes' trajectories, not specifically of a difference in size.
5. The covariate block of the record (design columns, reference row, missing policy, dropped participants) is reported beside the results. The adjustment is additive and linear on a covariate-blind basis, validated at three design columns and n = 80, and has no sex × covariate or stage × covariate term: the run offset is assumed equal in both sexes and across stages.
6. The p180 run offset (export manifest `counts.phase_offset`: median |d| 1.12, 80 of 133 metabolites above 1, PC1 correlation 0.73 with phase; NMR 0.21, 0, 0.13) and the block shares (p180 133 / 298, unweighted in MOTCO 0.10.0) are reported beside the results. A latent component that tracks phase is a reading matter for `axis-diagnostics` and the latent-adjusted arm, not an adjustment of the primary.
7. Feature membership and scale (`EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE`: 165 of 245 NMR columns, the floored log2, nothing imputed, winsorized or removed) are reported beside the results.
8. Reported runs use `n_jobs = 1`; a run at another value carries a finding and is not reported. Report numbers are pasted from the generated files and checked by `report --check`.
9. No result changes a constant. The analysis is run once at these settings; a second run at other settings is a new proposal.

### LATENT_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of the arm (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-latent-sensitivity` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. The arm is read beside the primary and never replaces it. MOTCO's power comparison returned `candidate_fails` for `stage` conditioning, which excludes the latent adjustment as a replacement of the covariate-blind primary.
2. The primary and the arm share the export, the covariates and their design, the cells, the CV folds, the seed and the permutations. They differ only in the latent basis and the CV-selected rank: the arm's basis is fitted on the features with the five covariates regressed out within stage, and the raw features are measured in it. A rejection status that differs is basis dependence, not a result of the arm.
3. The arm's CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. A rank that differs from the primary's is reported, not ranked as better or worse.
4. An `angle` rejection that appears only in the arm is read against MOTCO's controls, and is not evidence of an orientation difference on its own: the translation control rejected `angle` at 0.085–0.092 under `stage` conditioning, against the 0.0706 bound (the covariate-blind basis was above it too, at 0.076–0.086); under the Type I check, `angle` held the bound by only 0.004 (0.067 against 0.0706).
5. A `delta` or `shape` non-rejection in the arm is weaker than the primary's. MOTCO measured lost `shape` power with a covariate-aligned nuisance axis (0.243 against 0.428) and lost small-magnitude `delta` power without one (0.400 against 0.520 at e = 0.1). Reading rules 3 and 4 hold in the arm.
6. The arm's `latent_adjustment` block and its covariate block are reported beside its results. The `latent_adjustment` block holds the conditioning, the design columns, the residual df, the smallest CV training df and the feature R² summary; `feature_r2` states how much feature variance the residualization removed.
7. Residualizing a covariate that mediates the stage effect removes real stage signal, and no data can tell a confounder from a mediator. `age`, `education` and `apoe4` precede disease, and `phase` is the assay run and protocol (`LATENT_SENSITIVITY_COVARIATE_ROLES`). `statin` may follow diagnosis, so the arm's basis may have removed stage signal that runs through treatment.
8. MOTCO's latent-adjustment operating characteristics were measured at one design: n = 80, the SEA-AD MTG structure, 498 features, eight covariates in nine design columns, covariates confounded with group × stage far beyond this design's. None was measured at this design (n = 1501, 298 metabolites, six design columns), and the arm carries no Type I or power claim of its own.
9. A component of the arm that tracks a variable in its axis diagnostic is a measurement-space finding about the arm. It never reinterprets either analysis' statistics and is never grounds to adopt the arm.

### EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of an export variant (`EXPORT_VARIANT_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-nist-bridge` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. A variant is read beside the primary and never replaces it. No variant result changes a constant, reruns the primary or adopts a variant.
2. A variant shares with the primary the cleaned tables, the participants, the NMR block, the covariates and their design, the cells, the settings, the CV folds, the seed and the permutations. It differs only in the p180 values. A rejection status that differs is dependence on the handling of the p180 run, not a result of the variant.
3. The variant's CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. A rank that differs from the primary's is reported, not ranked.
4. `nist_bridge` removes the part of the run offset that NIST SRM 1950 carries, between-run calibration. It assumes the run acts as one factor per metabolite, the same in plasma (NIST) and serum (participants). It leaves what NIST cannot see: a shift common to most metabolites, sample-handling differences between the protocols (the largest residuals are aspartate and glutamate) and the seven unbridged metabolites. `phase` stays a covariate of the stage means and carries what remains.
5. `per_run_standardized` removes each p180 metabolite's whole difference in mean and spread between the runs, whatever its source: calibration, sample handling, and the stage-composition difference the runs carry, since SMC and EMCI were enrolled only in ADNI-GO/2. A difference between it and `nist_bridge` is read as the part of the run difference that is not calibration, never as evidence for either variant.
6. A non-rejection in a variant is no more informative than the primary's: reading rule 3 holds, and no power statement exists at this design. A rejection that appears only in a variant is dependence on the run handling and is read beside the variant's rank and null summary.
7. The variant's export manifest records the run offset per block, and for `nist_bridge` the factor per metabolite; both are reported beside the results. A variant component that tracks a variable in its axis diagnostic is a measurement-space finding and never grounds to adopt the variant.

### NMR_MEMBERSHIP_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of the NMR membership variant (`NMR_MEMBERSHIP_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-nmr-all-columns` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. `nmr_all_columns` is read beside the primary and never replaces it. No result of it changes a constant (`EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE` included), reruns the primary or adopts the variant.
2. The variant shares a great deal with the primary: the cleaned tables, the participants, the p180 block, the 165 NMR concentrations with their floors and cells, the covariates and their design, the cells, the settings, the CV folds, the seed and the permutations. It differs only in the NMR block's membership. It adds the 80 columns `EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE` marks `derived`, so the joint matrix holds 378 features, and the p180 share falls from 133 / 298 (0.446) to 133 / 378 (0.352). A rejection status that differs is dependence on the feature membership, not a result of the variant.
3. The variant's CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. A rank that differs from the primary's is reported, not ranked.
4. The 80 columns add no measurement. Each is a function of the block's concentrations: a lipid's share of its lipoprotein subclass (72 percentages), a ratio (4), the degree of unsaturation, or a particle size (3). On the rule's scale the 165 concentrations explain a median 0.998 of a derived column's variance (lowest 0.798). MOTCO z-scores every feature and weights features, not blocks, so the columns add weight to the lipoprotein directions they are computed from. On the rule's scale, 21 of them have |skew| > 1 and 8 a largest |z| above 8, mostly percentages of the large HDL and VLDL lipids. A difference reflects the weight of those directions and those tails, never evidence that the derived columns measure what the concentrations do not.
5. The 2022 study (doi:10.3389/fradi.2022.782864) used all 245 NMR columns, with `log2(x + 1)`, winsorizing, outlier removal and medication residuals. The variant changes the membership only, on the rule's scale. It is not a reproduction of that study, and no agreement or disagreement with it is read from the variant.
6. A non-rejection in the variant is no more informative than the primary's: reading rule 3 holds, and no power statement exists at this design. A rejection that appears only in the variant is dependence on the feature membership, read beside the variant's rank and null summary.
7. The variant's export manifest records the block shares, the NMR block's shape counts and the run offset per block. Its axis diagnostic records each block's loading share per component (`components.csv`). They are reported beside the results. A variant component that tracks a variable in its axis diagnostic is a measurement-space finding and never grounds to adopt the variant.
8. The variant is read against the primary only, never against another export variant, arm or rank. No sentence reads it against another sensitivity.

### SCALE_VARIANT_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of a scale variant (`SCALE_VARIANT_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-scale-variants` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. A scale variant is read beside the primary and never replaces it. No result of a scale variant changes a constant (the scale of `EXPORT_FEATURE_RULE` included), reruns the primary or adopts a variant.
2. A scale variant shares with the primary the cleaned tables, the participants, the membership of both blocks (133 p180 metabolites, 165 NMR concentrations), the covariates and their design, the cells, the settings, the CV folds, the seed and the permutations. It differs only in the scale of the cell values of both blocks. Every scale is a monotone function of the concentration, so each participant's rank on each feature is the primary's; what changes is the spacing of the values, which MOTCO's z-scoring passes to the basis and the statistics. A rejection status that differs is dependence on the scale, not a result of the variant.
3. The variant's CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. A rank that differs from the primary's is reported, not ranked.
4. `log2_plus_one` and `glog` keep the log and change its offset. The primary's offset is the metabolite's 5 % quantile (floor over median: p180 median 0.60, NMR 0.65), which compresses the lowest values of every metabolite alike. `log2_plus_one` is the transform of the 2022 study (doi:10.3389/fradi.2022.782864): its offset is 1 in each platform's unit (p180 µM, NMR mostly mmol/L), so an abundant metabolite gets nearly `log2(x)` and a scarce one a nearly linear scale (median below 1: 46 of 133 p180 metabolites, 129 of 165 NMR concentrations). It is not a reproduction of that study, whose membership, winsorizing, outlier removal and medication residuals differ, and no agreement or disagreement with it is read from the variant. `glog` is the generalized log with λ a tenth of the metabolite's smallest positive value: `log2(x)` within 0.004 bits on every positive cell, a zero cell 4.3 bits below the smallest positive value (41 NMR cells in 3 metabolites), and no compression of the low values, so it carries left tails (|skew| > 1 for 6 p180 and 25 NMR metabolites, a largest |z| above 8 for 6 and 13).
5. `raw_scale` takes the cleaned concentrations with no log. MOTCO z-scores each feature, so the right tails of the concentration distributions carry leverage (|skew| > 1 for 46 p180 and 43 NMR metabolites, a largest |z| above 8 for 12 and 4, against 1 and 3, 0 and 1 on the rule's scale), and the NMR block loses numerical rank (104 of 165; the joint matrix 237 of 298): the lipoprotein measures are exact sums of their components on the concentration scale, a relation the log breaks. A difference reflects those tails and that geometry, never evidence that one scale measures what another does not.
6. A non-rejection in a variant is no more informative than the primary's: reading rule 3 holds, and no power statement exists at this design. A rejection that appears only in a variant is dependence on the scale, read beside the variant's rank and null summary.
7. The variant's export manifest records the shape counts of each block on its scale, the numerical rank of each block, the run offset per block and the offset of each metabolite (the feature tables). They are reported beside the results. The run offset barely moves with the scale (p180 median |d| 1.08 to 1.13 against the primary's 1.12). A variant component that tracks a variable in its axis diagnostic is a measurement-space finding and never grounds to adopt the variant.
8. A scale variant is read against the primary only, never against another scale variant, another export variant, an arm or a rank. No sentence reads it against another sensitivity, and a difference between two scale variants is not evidence for either.

### STAGE_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of the CN / LMCI / AD arm (`STAGE_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-stage-sensitivity` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. The arm is read beside the primary and never replaces it. No arm result changes a constant, `STAGE_RULE` or `COHORT_RULE`, reruns the primary or adopts the arm.
2. The arm's rows are the primary export's rows of the 1130 participants whose `DX.bl` is CN, LMCI or AD, with the cells unchanged (the primary's floors). The covariates, their coding and every analysis setting are the primary's. It differs in the participants: the 96 SMC and 275 EMCI participants, enrolled only in ADNI-GO/2, are dropped. Its CN is `DX.bl` CN and its MCI is LMCI. Its z-scoring, CV folds, latent basis, rank, covariate reference row and permutation draws are its own. A rejection status that differs is dependence on the stage definition and the sample, not a result of the arm.
3. The arm's CV-selected rank, floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. A rank that differs from the primary's is reported, not ranked.
4. In the arm each stage holds one `DX.bl` category in both runs, so the `phase` coefficient of the stage-means model is estimated from contrasts within one diagnosis. In the primary, the ADNI-GO/2 rows of CN and MCI add SMC and EMCI. The arm neither balances the runs across stages nor removes the run offset, which is measurement: the ADNI-1 share per stage is CN 0.527, MCI 0.700, AD 0.544 in the arm and 0.417, 0.446, 0.544 in the primary; the p180 median |d| is 1.10 on the arm's rows and 1.12 on the primary's, and PC1's correlation with phase 0.71 and 0.73. `phase` stays a covariate under the additive assumption of reading rule 5.
5. The arm's MCI (LMCI) is a later and narrower stage than the primary's MCI, and its CN excludes subjective memory concern. A difference that involves the middle stage is a difference of stage definition. `angle` reads only the endpoint stages: the arm's AD cells are the primary's, and its CN cells lose the SMC participants.
6. The arm holds 1130 of the 1501 participants. Its non-rejection is no more informative than the primary's: reading rule 3 holds, and no power statement exists at this design. A rejection that appears only in the arm is dependence on the stage definition and the sample, read beside its rank and null summary.
7. The arm's export manifest records its cells, the runs within each stage and the run offset on its rows, and they are reported beside the results with the primary's. A component of the arm that tracks a variable in its axis diagnostic is a measurement-space finding about the arm and never grounds to adopt it. In the arm, `dx_bl` orders the participants as `stage` does, so their correlations coincide.
8. The arm is not crossed with the latent adjustment or an export variant, and is read against the primary only. No sentence reads it against another sensitivity.

### RANK_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES

Binding for every reader of the rank sweep (`RANK_SENSITIVITY_READING_RULES`, decided by `adni-rank-sensitivity` before any run), after `READING_RULES`:

1. The sweep is read beside the primary and never replaces it. The primary's rank is its CV-selected rank (8). No rank of the sweep is chosen, adopted or ranked, and no sweep result changes a constant (`ANALYSIS_RANK_FLOOR` and the ladder included), reruns the primary or defines another arm.
2. Every rank is the primary's analysis with one setting changed: `forced_components`, which fixes the rank and skips MOTCO's cross-validation. The export, the covariates and their coding, the seed, the permutations and every other setting are the primary's, and at `n_jobs = 1` every rank permutes the same residual indices. A difference between ranks is a difference of measurement, not of permutation draws. A rejection status that differs from the primary's is rank dependence, not a result at that rank.
3. PLS-DA components are nested. At a forced rank at or below the primary's 8, the latent space is spanned by the primary's first components; a rank above 8 adds components the cross-validation did not select. The anchor run at rank 8 reproduces the primary's record and files exactly, so the primary row is also the sweep's row at its rank.
4. Each rank's floor, eigengap and `angle` null summary are reported beside its `angle`. The `angle` null depends on the rank, so each `angle` is read against its own null, never against the `angle` of another rank. At rank 2, the floor, `angle` and `shape` are measured in two dimensions (reading rule 2).
5. MOTCO's forced-rank evidence is its Phase 5 rank ladder (2026-09-08; MOTCO 0.6.0, n = 1200, four stages, no covariates): size held at every forced rank, and the ranks above `n_stages − 1` lost `shape` power and gained no `angle` power. No Type I or power check exists at a forced rank at this design or with covariates. A non-rejection at any rank is no more informative than the primary's (reading rule 3), and a rejection at a forced rank only is rank dependence, read beside that rank's null summary.
6. The sweep is not a test. No rule combines the ranks, picks the rank with the smallest p-value or counts the ranks that reject, and the p-values are not adjusted for multiplicity because no decision is taken from them.
7. The axis diagnostic of the top rank (12) is reported beside the sweep. Its components up to the primary's rank are the primary's, with the primary's correlations. A component above it that tracks a variable, `phase` in particular, is a measurement-space finding about the higher ranks and never grounds to choose a rank.
8. The sweep is not crossed with the latent adjustment, an export variant or the CN / LMCI / AD arm, and is read against the primary only. The CV-selected ranks of those analyses are their own selections, and no sentence reads them against the sweep.

## Reproduction

The pipeline, in order, each step reading the previous steps' outputs and checking their manifests' hashes; `clean`, `cohort` and `export-sensitivity --variant nist_bridge` need the data root (`--data-root`, `ADNI_DATA_ROOT` or `data/`; the QC archives `ADMC_supplement.zip`, `ADMCDUKEP180SUPP_20170410.zip` are read in place, through top-level symlinks the data root must hold), the others read `results/` only, and `report` reads committed aggregate files only, so it runs on any clone:

```
uv run adni-sexdiff clean --platform all
uv run adni-sexdiff cohort
uv run adni-sexdiff export
uv run adni-sexdiff analyze
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff latent-sensitivity
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --analysis-dir results/motco_latent_sensitivity/analysis --out-dir results/motco_latent_sensitivity/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff export-sensitivity --variant all
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nist_bridge/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/per_run_standardized/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nmr_all_columns/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nmr_all_columns/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/nmr_all_columns/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/raw_scale/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/log2_plus_one/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/log2_plus_one/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/log2_plus_one/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/glog/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/glog/analysis --out-dir results/motco_export_sensitivity/glog/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff stage-sensitivity
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --input-dir results/motco_stage_sensitivity/motco_input --analysis-dir results/motco_stage_sensitivity/analysis --out-dir results/motco_stage_sensitivity/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff rank-sensitivity
uv run adni-sexdiff axis-diagnostics --analysis-dir results/motco_rank_sensitivity/rank_12 --out-dir results/motco_rank_sensitivity/diagnostics
uv run adni-sexdiff report
uv run adni-sexdiff report --check
```

`report --check` reads the newest `reports/motco-adni-<date>.md` (or the file `--out` names), reruns every gate, regenerates the report from the outputs now on disk with the date taken from the file name and compares every table cell by cell and every other line line by line; a regenerated results directory fails it on the provenance hashes until a new report is written and checked.

MOTCO's CLI equivalents of the 15 analyses, the primary, the arm, `nist_bridge`, `per_run_standardized`, `nmr_all_columns`, `raw_scale`, `log2_plus_one`, `glog`, the CN / LMCI / AD arm `cn_lmci_ad` and the 6 forced ranks of the rank sweep `rank_02`, `rank_03`, `rank_04`, `rank_06`, `rank_09`, `rank_12` (`cli_equivalent` of their analysis manifests):
